Fixed in `ops/keyword_demand.py`'s `_page_headings()`: a heading whose entire content is a single `<a href="...">...</a>` is now excluded from a page's own `head_words`, the same "not this page's own content" reasoning the 2026-11-11 band-heading fix (entry 40 in the test file) already uses for a promotional section. | **Phil was receiving 2-3 duplicate copies of the morning LinkedIn/Bluesky/Facebook-X drafting emails on most days, found and fixed, 2026-10-13, scheduled operator.** | Every unblocked row below was again Done or Phil-gated, GitHub's 9 issues unchanged (all decision/blocked-on-art), no mail credential, so this cycle checked the one thing the standing cold-read ledger could not: whether the distribution workflows this `GOALS.md` section names as "the only channel that already works" (LinkedIn) actually deliver once a day as designed. Confirmed directly from git history, not assumed: `git log --grep='LinkedIn drafts: advance rotation'` shows 8 of the last 28 days carrying 2 or 3 real send commits in one day, including every day from 2026-10-09 to 2026-10-12 straight; `bluesky-drafts.yml` and `social-drafts.yml` share the identical shape and the identical pattern (8 of 13, 8 of 28 days respectively). Root cause: each workflow's gate step treats any non-push trigger (`$EVENT_NAME" != 'push'`) as an automatic send, on the theory cron fires at most once a day. It does not fire ON TIME once a day (`gate_scheduled_delivery_phase` already measures linkedin-drafts.yml landing 156 minutes past its own promise); when the delayed schedule run finally executes AFTER the push fallback has already stood in for it, both send, because the schedule branch never checked the once-a-day ledger the push branch does. Fixed in all three workflows: only `workflow_dispatch` (a deliberate manual run) now sends unconditionally; `schedule` falls through to the same "already sent today" commit-ledger check as `push`. Simulated all six gate branches directly (schedule-on-time, schedule-delayed-after-a-push-send, push-before-target, push-fallback-empty-ledger, push-fallback-already-sent, manual-dispatch) before shipping, confirmed each produces the correct `send=yes`/`send=no`. New `gate_schedule_fallback_race` in `preflight.py`, fail-then-pass proved directly against a planted copy of the old pattern and against the real committed files; `ops/tests/test_gate_schedule_fallback_race.py` (7/7 cases, including the real committed workflows). No production code bug in `deploy.py` itself (`compose_drift` only reports, never affects the exit code), so no behaviour changed for the one credentialed session that can ever run this file for real; this closes a test-quality gap in the one script guarding production honesty, not a live defect. `gate_tests()` already globs every `ops/tests/test_*.py` file, so no new `preflight.py` gate was needed, matching this file's own established precedent for the `ops/ship.py` and `render_all_zone_videos.py` exit-code fixes. | **The executive dashboard's own headline sentence went stale the moment a real fix landed, because nothing recorded a confirmed live deploy anywhere durable; fixed structurally, 2026-09-14 operator.** A same-day local session with real VPS/Stripe/mail access (`5bc41464`) deployed and confirmed production current by build id, then, in the same pass, edited `site/standards.html`, which moved the repo's own build id on again before ever rerunning `ops/dashboard.py`. `ops/state.json` still held `deploy_last_verdict: "stale"` from before that deploy, and this sandbox has no egress either, so simply regenerating the dashboard here would have carried that same now-superseded "stale" forward, with no way to tell "never redeployed in weeks" apart from "one file behind since an hour ago" (the exact ambiguity CLAUDE.md 0.3/0.4 warns against). `ops/deploy.py` is the only file that ever runs with real access to production, and it proved a real deploy live, then said nothing durable about it. Fixed by having it write `ops/deploy-verdict.json` (verdict, build id, timestamp) the instant it confirms a build live, and by teaching `ops/dashboard.py`'s `resolve_deploy_verdict()` to read that marker when this run has no live measurement of its own: current if the repo's build id still matches the marker, honestly "stale, N-vs-M build ids, not yet redeployed since a confirmed deploy at <time>" if it has since moved on. Found and fixed two follow-on bugs while proving this: the marker branch first reused this run's own unmeasured 0/0 asset counts instead of the real carried "1 of 9," recreating the exact 2026-08-31 bug this file's own docstring warns against, fixed by routing every branch through one shared `_carried_counts()` helper; and a strict `>` timestamp comparison caused the honest note to appear once and then silently vanish on the very next regeneration (the marker's timestamp, once carried into `prev`, was no longer "newer"), fixed to `>=`. New `gate_dashboard_deploy_marker_carry_forward` in `ops/preflight.py` (7 synthetic cases: fresh matching marker resolves current; a drifted marker names both build ids and reads stale; an older marker cannot regress a fresher carry; a real live measurement always wins; the marker re-applies idempotently on repeat runs), fail-then-pass proved directly against the pre-fix `resolve_deploy_verdict()` signature (`TypeError`, no `marker` parameter existed). `ops/tests/test_deploy.py` extended 14 to 20 cases covering `write_verdict_marker()` (a confirmed deploy persists build id and timestamp; a failed or no-op check never writes one). | **LinkedIn drafts had been repeating the identical three posts to Phil every morning for 9 days, and the sibling Facebook/X mailer had never once advanced either, both silently violating `ops/corpus_posts.py`'s own "nothing repeats until the pool is exhausted" guarantee; fixed and gated, 2026-09-15 operator.** Every unblocked row in sections 2-6 was again done or Phil-gated, the 7 open GitHub issues had no new reply, and the low-mention `ops/*.py` cold-read lane had itself been independently confirmed exhausted (minimum 8 mentions across all 139 files) by several of today's own prior cycles, so this pass tried a different instrument: reading a same-day PM check-in's own handoff ("open the 3 generated drafts and read them for real... nobody has checked this specific day's actual draft content since the reflow.py fix") rather than the content itself, and asking why nobody ever had. Traced rather than assumed: `ops/linkedin_drafts.py --send` calls `corpus_posts.take(..., record=True)`, which writes `ops/corpus-rotation.json` to disk, but `git log --all` on that file shows exactly two commits, both by Phil directly, the last 2026-09-06; no automated commit has ever touched it. Neither `.github/workflows/linkedin-drafts.yml` nor `.github/workflows/social-drafts.yml` contains a `git commit`/`git push` step at all (confirmed by grep, zero hits in either file), and both ran with `permissions: contents: read`, which could not push even if a step tried. Selection in `take()` is purely deterministic on the served-set (`sorted(fresh, key=lambda x: (chapter, title))[:n]`, no date or randomness), so every GitHub Actions run starts from the same committed baseline and produces the same three posts. Confirmed this was not a theoretical gap: pulled real workflow-job step logs directly from the GitHub API for `linkedin-drafts.yml`'s scheduled (non-push) runs on 2026-09-09/10/12/13/14, every one's "Write three drafts and send them" step completed `success`, not skipped, meaning each was a genuine send of the identical batch ("Your room is not a messy person", "Borrow the logic, not the look", "It came from a car factory"); `social-drafts.yml` showed the identical shape across its 3 real scheduled sends (2026-09-12/13/14), and its rotation file carried no `facebook-post` or `x-post` key at all, meaning those two channels had never advanced even once. Fixed both workflows with a `permissions.contents: write` bump and a commit-and-push step mirroring `hourly-brief.yml`'s own already-proven pattern (`git add`, `git diff --cached --quiet || git commit`, `git pull --rebase --autostash origin main || true`, `git push origin HEAD:main || true`), gated behind the same `steps.gate.outputs.send == 'yes'` condition the send step already uses. Corrected `ops/corpus-rotation.json` itself to mark the batch Phil has already received (repeatedly) as served, via the real `corpus_posts.take(..., record=True)` call, so the fix advances rotation forward rather than repeating that exact batch one more time before finally moving. New `gate_draft_rotation_persisted` in `ops/preflight.py` (pure logic in `check_draft_rotation_persisted`), checking both workflows for `permissions.contents: write` and a step that references `ops/corpus-rotation.json` together with a `git push`, so a future edit that drops either half regresses loudly instead of silently; `ops/tests/test_gate_draft_rotation_persisted.py` (6 cases: both fixed, permission missing, persist step entirely absent, a push step that pushes the wrong file, a missing workflow file, and the real committed files) fail-then-pass proved directly (`git stash` on the two workflow files reproduced both real pre-fix failures by name). Full `preflight.py` (every gate passed, 23 warnings, all previously diagnosed sandbox limits) after. No price or product touched, no site page changed, IndexNow not applicable: this is a scheduled-mailer persistence bug, not a published page. Not verifiable from this sandbox at the time: an actual GitHub Actions run of the new commit-and-push step (no workflow-trigger access here); correctness verified by direct code and YAML review plus the fail-then-pass gate, not by watching a real run succeed, and flagged honestly rather than claimed. **Verified live, 2026-09-15 PM check-in:** the next real scheduled run of each workflow did push. `linkedin-drafts.yml`'s 15:16:28 UTC schedule run committed `09555683` ("LinkedIn drafts: advance rotation", author `github-actions`) three new served ids into the `linkedin-post` list. `social-drafts.yml`'s 17:43:17 UTC schedule run committed `14a9498c` the same way into both `facebook-post` and `x-post`, the two channels that had never advanced before this fix. Confirmed by reading the real commits and their diffs, not inferred from a green run: the fix is holding in production. |

Classified all five (`corpus_index.py`), extending `units_in()` with a `discussion-questions` rule (a plain numbered list, no bold lead) verified against the real corpus, e.g. Chapter 33's 20 real questions counted as 20, not 1. **Caught before shipping, not after:** the same defect a third time, this time worse than a zero yield. `corpus_posts.py`'s `EXTRACTORS` dict had no entry for any of the four new kinds, so they would have fallen back to `split_posts`'s numbered-`## `-heading shape, which none of them use, and 42 of 51 `back-cover-copy.md` files (over three-quarters of the merged "sales-copy" kind) carry a trailing pull-quote section under one of at least six different headings ("Testimonials", "Pull Testimonials", "Pull Testimonials (PLACEHOLDERS)", some `##`, one `###`) holding unfilled scaffolding: `[PLACEHOLDER TESTIMONIAL 1: ...]` blockquotes or `PLACEHOLDER: reader testimonial about...` bullets, explicitly marked "do not publish as real quotes." Marking the kind ready without an extractor that understood this shape would have been a live fabricated-testimonial risk the moment anything served it, not just an empty pool, so this was checked before the classifier change was shipped, not discovered after. New `split_sales_copy` cuts at the first heading whose text contains "testimonial", any level, any wording, then refuses the whole file if the word "placeholder" survives anywhere in what is left rather than trust the cut caught every shape (3 of 51 files still fail that net, on a pre-heading disclaimer line, and are correctly dropped rather than served with the disclaimer attached); `split_short` and `split_questions` cover the other three kinds.
